     - Added process-conditional BR decomposition paragraph after line 47:
     ```latex
     \looseness=-1 Process-conditional decomposition reveals that SCB is not a local artifact of answer leakage: BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$), confirming that bias is present from the first extraction step. Conditioning on difficulty amplifies the pattern: high-consensus errors (SC $\geq 10/12$) exhibit $\text{BR} = 18.8\times$, versus $2.7\times$ for low-consensus errors (SC $\leq 6/12$; $p < 0.001$)---indicating near-complete capture of FOL extraction by incorrect reasoning when the model is most confident.

        "content": "\\vspace{-1mm}\n\\section{Conclusion}\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We identify symbolic confirmation bias: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$, 83.3\\%), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$/$+14.22$\\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the bottleneck is extraction quality, not the formal channel. Three diagnostics predict failure ($C_0 < 5\\%$, SC $\\leq 40\\%$, process similarity). Cross-architecture NLI escapes the bias when the verifier exceeds the generator ($p = 0.003$); DRC recovers 83--90\\% of this gain at 35--39\\% cost across two datasets.\n",

        "startLine": 1,

        "content": "\\vspace{-1mm}\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-verification and BR magnitude.}\n\\looseness=-1 Our bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification, chen2025sets}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. That $\\text{BR} > 1$ is expected from self-extraction; the finding is the magnitude---nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems, consistent across three families. Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling.}\n\\looseness=-1 $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ \\citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles.\n\n\\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}\nTwo diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n",
        "numLines": 13,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 13
      }
    },

        "content": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Process-conditional decomposition reveals that SCB is not a local artifact of answer leakage: BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$), confirming that bias is present from the first extraction step. Conditioning on difficulty amplifies the pattern: high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) exhibit $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$, versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus errors (SC $\\leq 6/12$; $p < 0.001$)---indicating near-complete capture of FOL extraction by incorrect reasoning when the model is most confident.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",

        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 79

        "content": "\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\nWe evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters).\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n\n\n\\subsection{Main Results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all conditions sorted by SC baseline, revealing three observation-driven accuracy regimes.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all conditions (sorted by SC). No non-degenerate condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; $\\dagger$\\,$n < 100$; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$); {***}\\,oracle $p < 0.001$; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory (unmarked: single-seed exploratory).}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--55\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 51.00 & 51.50 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & 59.31 & $+$3.43 & 0.092 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 54.41 & 53.92 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 75.17 & 72.67 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & 74.51 & $-$0.98 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 78.50 & 78.00 & $-$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 84.80 & 84.31 & $-$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.00 & 85.67 & $+$0.67 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Degenerate ($C_0 < 5\\%$)}} \\\\\nQwQ-32B & AIME 2024$^\\dagger$ & 22 & 72.70 & 72.70 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & GSM8K$^\\dagger$ & 30 & 96.67 & 96.67 & 0.00 & \\textrm{---} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Oracle upper bound (gold logical annotations + Z3)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 55.88 & \\textbf{85.78} & $\\mathbf{+29.90}$ & $1.0{\\times}10^{-13}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.49 & \\textbf{89.71} & $\\mathbf{+14.22}$ & $2.0{\\times}10^{-7}${***} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & ProofWriter & 600 & 75.17 & \\textbf{86.67} & $\\mathbf{+11.50}$ & $9.21{\\times}10^{-15}${***} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\looseness=-1 No non-degenerate condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); adjusted $p = 0.112$ for both. The intermediate-SC region (50--55\\%) contains the only positive trends, none reaching $p < 0.05$. Four-seed replication confirms seed-level noise: two of four seeds produce null or negative $\\Delta$, and the aggregate ($+3.43$\\,pp) does not reach significance ($p = 0.092$). TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp confirm 4 equivalent, 2 genuinely different (both negative: Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter), 10 underpowered---reflecting the power limitations at $n{=}200$--$204$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). The $\\pm 2$\\,pp margin corresponds to the lower bound of gains reported in the SC literature \\citep{wang2023sc} (2--5\\,pp); $\\pm 1$\\,pp yields 0 equivalent conditions (0/2/14), $\\pm 3$\\,pp yields 10 (10/0/6). This absolute margin is more conservative at high baselines ($\\pm 2.4\\%$ relative at 85\\%) than low ones ($\\pm 5.1\\%$ at 39\\%), working against our equivalence claim where SICA most plausibly helps. We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\nReplacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling.\n\n\n",
        "numLines": 73,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 73
      }

```text
1	\vspace{-1mm}
2	\section{Discussion}
3	\label{sec:discussion}
4	
5	\paragraph{Self-verification and BR magnitude.}
6	\looseness=-1 Our bias ratio ($4.97\times$ Mistral, $3.93\times$ Qwen, $2.88\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification, chen2025sets}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. That $\text{BR} > 1$ is expected from self-extraction; the finding is the magnitude---nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems, consistent across three families. Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.
7	
8	\paragraph{Test-time scaling.}
9	\looseness=-1 $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$ \citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles.
10	
11	\paragraph{Practitioner implications.}
12	Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp).
13	
```

</details>

4	\looseness=-1 We identify symbolic confirmation bias: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\%), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Three diagnostics predict failure ($C_0 < 5\%$, SC $\leq 40\%$, process similarity). Cross-architecture NLI escapes the bias when the verifier exceeds the generator ($p = 0.003$); DRC recovers 83--90\% of this gain at 35--39\% cost across two datasets.

  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Test-time scaling and practitioner implications.}\n\\looseness=-1 $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp).     "originalFile": "\\vspace{-1mm}\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-verification and BR magnitude.}\n\\looseness=-1 Our bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification, chen2025sets}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. The finding is the magnitude---nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems, consistent across three families. Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling and practitioner implications.}\n\\looseness=-1 $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ \\citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles. Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n",

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\ $p = 6.78 \times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\ $p = 7.41 \times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	Three conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\S\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \textsc{False} (71.43\% vs.\ 38.10\%), SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
13	
14	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
15	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.
16	
17	\begin{table}[t]
18	\centering
19	\small
20	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}
21	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
22	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
23	\toprule
24	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
25	\midrule
26	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
27	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.66 \\
28	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.50 \\
29	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.45 \\
30	\bottomrule
31	\end{tabular}}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}
35	\label{sec:drc}
36	
37	Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\tau$; above $\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.
38	
39	\begin{table}[t]
40	\centering
41	\small
42	\caption{DRC on Mistral-7B ($K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli, $w{=}3$). $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 83--90\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\% NLI cost across both datasets.}
43	\label{tab:drc}
44	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
45	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
46	\toprule
47	Dataset & Method & Acc.\ & NLI Calls & Cost & Recovery \\
48	\midrule
49	\multirow{3}{*}{PW-D5} & SC only & 39.33\% & 0/600 & 0\% & --- \\
50	& \textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{45.33\%} & \textbf{233/600} & \textbf{38.8\%} & \textbf{90.0\%} \\
51	& Full combo & 46.00\% & 600/600 & 100\% & 100\% \\
52	\midrule
53	\multirow{3}{*}{FOLIO} & SC only & 55.88\% & 0/204 & 0\% & --- \\
54	& \textbf{DRC $\tau{=}0.7$} & \textbf{58.33\%} & \textbf{71/204} & \textbf{34.8\%} & \textbf{83.3\%} \\
55	& Full combo & 58.82\% & 204/204 & 100\% & 100\% \\
56	\bottomrule
57	\end{tabular}
58	\end{table}
59	
60	\looseness=-1 DRC at $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\% of full-combo gain on ProofWriter ($+6.00$ out of $+6.67$\,pp) at 38.8\% NLI cost, and 83.3\% on held-out FOLIO ($+2.45$ out of $+2.94$\,pp) at 34.8\% cost---confirming that per-question agreement routing generalizes without re-tuning $\tau$ (selected on ProofWriter; FOLIO is held-out).
61	
62	
```

    "newString": "\\enlargethispage{\\baselineskip}\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThree conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{DRC on Mistral-7B ($K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli, $w{=}3$). $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\\% NLI cost across both datasets.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nDataset & Method & Acc.\\ & NLI Calls & Cost & Recovery \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{PW-D5} & SC only & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} & \\textbf{90.0\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{FOLIO} & SC only & 55.88\\% & 0/204 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{58.33\\%} & \\textbf{71/204} & \\textbf{34.8\\%} & \\textbf{83.3\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 58.82\\% & 204/204 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of full-combo gain on ProofWriter ($+6.00$ out of $+6.67$\\,pp) at 38.8\\% NLI cost, and 83.3\\% on held-out FOLIO ($+2.45$ out of $+2.94$\\,pp) at 34.8\\% cost---confirming that per-question agreement routing generalizes without re-tuning $\\tau$ (selected on ProofWriter; FOLIO is held-out).\n\n",

          " Three conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).",

After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The finding is the magnitude---nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems, consistent across three families. Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling and practitioner implications.}\n\\looseness=-1 $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ \\citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles. Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n",

{
  "parentUuid": "0b401797-5f2f-445c-9f73-acf459238551",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

[Director 指令] [Patch] BAF-Decoupled 实验结果整合

exp-d134-baf-decoupled 完成，结果需整合到论文。

**实验设计**：截断 CoT（去掉 answer sentence），用截断后的 trace 提取约束做 SICA，测试 SCB 是否来源于 answer leakage。三种截断策略：Conservative / Standard / Aggressive。

**关键结果**：
- 截断后 BR 无显著下降（Wilcoxon one-sided p > 0.12 across all strategies）
- 截断后 accuracy 显著下降（McNemar p < 0.013）
- 结论：**SCB 不是 answer leakage**，而是嵌入在整个推理过程中的结构性偏差

| 策略 | SICA Accuracy | BR Mean | Wilcoxon p (BR) | McNemar p (Acc) |
|------|--------------|---------|-----------------|-----------------|
| Full trace | 59.31% | 2.787 | — | — |
| Conservative | 53.92% | 2.490 | 0.122 | 0.001 |
| Standard | 54.41% | 2.655 | 0.546 | 0.006 |
| Aggressive | 53.92% | 2.255 | 0.256 | 0.013 |

**整合位置**：analysis.tex §5.2（紧接 Process-BR decomposition 段落之后），作为 SCB mechanism 的第二个证据。

**叙事**：Process-BR 证明 SCB 均匀分布于 early/mid/late steps，BAF-decoupled 进一步证明即使物理移除 answer sentence，SCB 依然存在 → SCB 来源于模型对问题的 distributional prior，不是简单的 answer 泄漏。

这次不用验证引用。编译确认 8 页限制。

1	\section{Analysis}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	We test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}).
5	
6	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
10	
11	\begin{table}[t]
12	\centering
13	\small
14	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
15	\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\kappa$ descending). $\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$, 4 traces each.}
16	\label{tab:independence}
17	\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}
18	\toprule
19	Model & Condition & $\kappa$ & $K_{\text{eff}}$ & SC (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
20	\midrule
21	Qwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\sim}$84 & ${\sim}$0 \\
22	Qwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\
23	Qwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\
24	Qwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\
25	Qwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\
26	\addlinespace
27	Mistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\
28	Mistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\
29	Mistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\
30	Mistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\
31	Mistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\
32	Mistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\
33	Mistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\
34	Mistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\
35	Mistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\
36	Mistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\
37	\bottomrule
38	\end{tabular}
39	\end{table}
40	
41	
42	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
43	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
44	
45	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}).
46	
47	\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes: $4.97\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.
48	
49	\looseness=-1 Process-conditional decomposition reveals that SCB is not a local artifact of answer leakage: BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$), confirming that bias is present from the first extraction step. Conditioning on difficulty amplifies the pattern: high-consensus errors (SC $\geq 10/12$) exhibit $\text{BR} = 18.8\times$, versus $2.7\times$ for low-consensus errors (SC $\leq 6/12$; $p < 0.001$)---indicating near-complete capture of FOL extraction by incorrect reasoning when the model is most confident.
50	
51	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
52	\label{sec:process-kappa}
53	
54	We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:
55	\begin{equation}
56	\label{eq:process-sim}
57	\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \sum_{i < j} \cos(\mathbf{v}_i^q, \mathbf{v}_j^q),
58	\end{equation}
59	where $\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks.
60	
61	
62	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
63	\label{sec:diagnostics}
64	
65	Further diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\sim}$10\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.
66	
67	
68	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
69	\label{sec:remediation}
70	\label{sec:interventions}
71	
72	\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \ref{tab:remediation}, \ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.
73	
74	\begin{figure*}[t]
75	\centering
76	\includegraphics[width=0.85\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}
77	\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\pm 1$\,pp noise zone.}
78	\label{fig:interventions-summary}
79	\end{figure*}
```

All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Process-conditional decomposition reveals that SCB is not a local artifact of answer leakage: BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$), confirming that bias is present from the first extraction step. Conditioning on difficulty amplifies the pattern: high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) exhibit $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$, versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus errors (SC $\\leq 6/12$; $p < 0.001$)---indicating near-complete capture of FOL extraction by incorrect reasoning when the model is most confident.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",
      "numLines": 79,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 79
    }

    "oldString": "\\looseness=-1 Process-conditional decomposition reveals that SCB is not a local artifact of answer leakage: BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$), confirming that bias is present from the first extraction step. Conditioning on difficulty amplifies the pattern: high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) exhibit $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$, versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus errors (SC $\\leq 6/12$; $p < 0.001$)---indicating near-complete capture of FOL extraction by incorrect reasoning when the model is most confident.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}",
    "newString": "\\looseness=-1 Process-conditional decomposition reveals that SCB is not a local artifact of answer leakage: BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$), confirming that bias is present from the first extraction step. Conditioning on difficulty amplifies the pattern: high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) exhibit $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$, versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus errors (SC $\\leq 6/12$; $p < 0.001$)---indicating near-complete capture of FOL extraction by incorrect reasoning when the model is most confident.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Answer-free extraction corroborates this finding. We truncate each CoT trace by removing the final answer sentence before constraint extraction (three strategies: conservative, standard, aggressive), testing whether SCB arises from explicit answer leakage. Across all strategies, BR shows no significant decrease (Wilcoxon one-sided $p > 0.12$; full-trace $\\text{BR} = 2.79$, truncated $\\text{BR} = 2.26$--$2.66$), while accuracy drops significantly (McNemar $p < 0.013$; $59.31\\% \\to 53.92$--$54.41\\%$). SCB thus reflects a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer-sentence contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Process-conditional decomposition reveals that SCB is not a local artifact of answer leakage: BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$), confirming that bias is present from the first extraction step. Conditioning on difficulty amplifies the pattern: high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) exhibit $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$, versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus errors (SC $\\leq 6/12$; $p < 0.001$)---indicating near-complete capture of FOL extraction by incorrect reasoning when the model is most confident.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Process-conditional decomposition reveals that SCB is not a local artifact of answer leakage: BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$), confirming that bias is present from the first extraction step. Conditioning on difficulty amplifies the pattern: high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) exhibit $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$, versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus errors (SC $\\leq 6/12$; $p < 0.001$)---indicating near-complete capture of FOL extraction by incorrect reasoning when the model is most confident.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Answer-free extraction corroborates this finding. We truncate each CoT trace by removing the final answer sentence before constraint extraction (three strategies: conservative, standard, aggressive), testing whether SCB arises from explicit answer leakage. Across all strategies, BR shows no significant decrease (Wilcoxon one-sided $p > 0.12$; full-trace $\\text{BR} = 2.79$, truncated $\\text{BR} = 2.26$--$2.66$), while accuracy drops significantly (McNemar $p < 0.013$; $59.31\\% \\to 53.92$--$54.41\\%$). SCB thus reflects a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer-sentence contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks.\n\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",
